# Manifest

What we do and where the practice comes from. Nothing enters the harness without a row. A practice we stop using is deleted; git keeps the history. `just prove` runs every proof whose claim holds in a host checkout; the devcontainer build runs the container proofs and the maintainer runs the GitHub-settings proofs with the admin token. CI publishes the count.

| Read-only one-socket feed tap and capacity gate | alpacahq/alpaca-py@v0.44.0 `cc4cb3b7ba50ae250e621983c2779047fb16bb28`: `alpaca/data/live/websocket.py:324-372` (`_dispatch` awaits the subscribed handler on every message, the per-message service boundary the tap times), `:421-491` (`_run_forever` reconnects inside `run()` without a handler callback, so a reconnect is never a counted event), `alpaca/data/live/stock.py:14-58` (`StockDataStream`, `data_timeout` disabled by default); Mann (1945), "Nonparametric tests against trend", Econometrica 13(3):245-259, https://doi.org/10.2307/1907187, and Kendall (1975), Rank Correlation Methods, 4th ed., tie-corrected normal approximation; [statistics.NormalDist](https://docs.python.org/3.13/library/statistics.html#statistics.NormalDist) read 2026-11-10; the six pre-registered criteria, the analyzer and the plan are preserved on ref `research/feed-capacity-df9b54a` at annotated tag `feed-capacity-2026-11-10b` (head `754c7a049b89a08bb5139893ecbab07c21a3d800`, cut from `df9b54a`), which carries `src/sextant/paper/feed_capacity.py`, its proofs and `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-10-feed-tap.md` | `FeedTap` counts entries at `AlpacaTickFeed`'s existing SIP handlers (`src/sextant/paper/alpaca_feed.py:270-302`) with integer counters and writes one JSON line per closed wall-clock second beside the session receipt (`<dir>/feed-tap.jsonl`: trade and quote counts, distinct symbols, receipt-lag count/p99/p99.9/max taken at handler entry, handler service sum/max, failures) then one aggregate line (symbol count, symbols with a measurable gap, longest gap, unobserved reconnects, dropped/write/init counters); no ticker name is written. The handler path takes no lock and does no I/O: closed seconds go to a bounded queue one daemon writer drains, an overflow, a write error or a sidecar that cannot open is counted and never stops the feed, and the tap finalizes after `stream.run()` returns. The tap is opt-in (`AlpacaTickFeed(tap=...)`), default OFF byte-identically, adds no socket, subscription or retry, and is refused with BOATS. The six-criterion gate (`feed_capacity.py`) is study code and runs from the tagged ref against a recorded sidecar; its `entry_input_max_age_s` has no registry row, so it has no default | `tests/proofs/test_feed_tap.py::test_a_feed_without_the_tap_delivers_identically_and_reads_the_clock_once`, `tests/proofs/test_feed_tap.py::test_the_sidecar_writes_each_closed_second_then_the_symbols_line` |

A spelling is added to the table and regenerated, never to a rule file, and `just ci` fails when a file is stale. `.claude/settings.json` `permissions.deny`: 189 `Bash` rules, for `wsl.exe`, a push whose refspec lands on `main`, `--all`, `--branches` and `--mirror`, `git worktree add`, `printenv`, every `env` call and `export -p`, each also by path and behind every wrapper Claude Code does not strip, with git's own options before the subcommand and a shell's `-c` script; and one `Edit` rule, `Edit(//mnt/wsl/**)`, which denies the whole shared mount to its file tools and to a redirection's or `tee`'s target, `/mnt/wsl/sextant` included: a named over-block. The fixture commands from the justfile and harness remain allowed by the dsh guard and documented Antigravity matcher, as `test_no_command_the_harness_runs_is_refused` checks; the Claude receipt separately checks the lane-command subset. Every `env` call is denied because `env NAME=value` with no command prints the environment and a glob cannot require a command: write `NAME=value command`. `crossSessionInbound: "hold"` waits until coordination moves to Orca messages; `autoMemoryEnabled` stays unset (ADR-0022). The rules match command text: a script that pushes inside passes, and main's ruleset stays the server-side guard. [ADR-0007](adr/0007-custody-and-order-writing.md):16-17 (house provenance), :21 (flat needs terminal lineage) and :27 (cancel K=3, at most K+1 per order); #129's reconciliation, §4 item 1; #169, #171, #209, #210 cause 1, #214's blind dsh leg (comment 6711932490) and its GPT trading gate and delta gates (comments 6712732527, 6713462191, 6714163408) | `src/sextant/exits/engine.py`: the house is what the engine's own `submit_order` sent, and a house order's fills grow the protection; `on_position_opened` protects only a house opener and reads the Decimal `Position.avg_px_open`; H moves only on trades; `_sell` records the crossing, freezes the levels, cancels each working house order once, sets a 60-second clock alert that pages an exit still owed, and sells when every reservation is terminal (cancel, expiry, rejection, denial or a fill read from the position); a close with a canceled reservation unfinished keeps its exit owed and sells any such reservation's late fill, and that kept cycle retires once its last reservation ends flat; a rejected cancel goes out again once 5 s have passed since that order's last cancel request on the engine's clock, while the order still works, up to K+1 cancels per order, then pages, and never removes the order from the crossing's reservations; no open position and no unfinished order, re-checked on each quote and terminal order event while paused and before a house opener, clears a pause; each page is one ERROR log line, once per reason in each position cycle, and each pause is recorded; an owner's sell is `owner_sell_fill`. | Kill-to-flat keeps one exit quantity in flight; a registration refused before the wire is released unsent (#123, #151) | seathatflowsinourveins/northstar-trading@paper `8256c16c31dcb8e53fc726e0c1c77dc2c8d0d4fa`: `src/nautilus_alpaca/http.py::RestCaller.call` (`before_send` runs once the pacer admits, right before the request leaves; a raise from it aborts the request), `order_reducer.py::_t_cancel_unsent` and `execution.py::AlpacaSubmitAborted` (a submit that never left is closed locally), and `northstar_trading/run.py:193-223` with `nodes/live.py:489-501` (the account lock is taken, then custody is restored from its file before any venue connects); nautechsystems/nautilus_trader@`d53849b216e20f2c122ca01b4dd0da377638b186` (2.0.0rc8): the Strategy API's OrderFactory builds and `submit_order`, `cancel_order` and `modify_order` send; `crates/execution/src/engine/mod.rs:2191-2205` routes a command only to a registered client (`OrderDeniedReason::NoExecutionClient` otherwise) and `:2524-2541` claims the order for it before sending; the RiskEngine's precision denial after `submit_order` and `OrderDenied`; the #104 reviews' fix shapes (custody's own book, not a second reservation book; a durable not-sent record); ADR-0007 one exit per quantity | The native layer only prepares: `PaperStrategy._prepare` validates (cache, identity, price and size precision and range; a client ID rc7 refuses fails at load) and builds one native command, and it never receives the accounting hook. `PaperRuntime` runs its own hook, then that command, in `RestCaller.call`'s order: it registers durably, rechecks admission with the own-ID exclusion, and lets row 8's pacer count the wire. Preparing runs in the pacer's admission, before its token: a `NativeRefusal` is quiet with no row and spends no token, and a refused kill EXIT waits for a new observation as an rc8 denial does; any other error there halts with no row. A kill EXIT is validated before any bookkeeping. A refusal or error in the hook releases the registration through custody's durable `release_unsent`, because the command never ran; an unexpected one also halts. So does an rc8 `OrderDenied` through `on_order_denied`, including the ExecutionEngine's denial of an order no registered client can take. Once the command runs, an error keeps the row in flight and halts. A release applies only to an unlinked row with a non-empty reason that no live report shows. A terminal, unfilled, venue-less report agrees with it, and any other report freezes the symbol. Released rows are written as version 2. The writer reloads custody when it (re)acquires the lease. Every admission check, including a queued kill EXIT's current-price check, runs before the pacer's token. An admission error halts and returns False, so the pacer pops the item. A symbol keeps one queued kill EXIT unless its price moved, and only a denied kill EXIT waits for a new observation. `pulse()` isolates each kill intent and always drains. Within the same pulse, one unknown instrument does not stop another's FLATTEN; the guard's account-wide stop at the next observation is #51's call. `denied()` releases only this writer's own row while it holds the lease. No shipped driver calls `pulse()` or `observe()` yet (`build_shell` is inert, #104): B's node supplies them through #139's reconcile and B's account slice's native clock timer, so until then kill-to-flat is proven in drills | `tests/proofs/test_paper_row8.py::test_kill_to_flat_keeps_one_exit_quantity_in_flight_across_ticks_and_a_pause`, `test_a_refusal_after_registration_leaves_kill_to_flat_working`, `test_a_refusal_inside_the_pacer_hook_is_released`, `test_a_repeated_native_refusal_writes_no_custody_on_any_tick`, `test_an_error_once_the_wire_is_counted_keeps_the_exit_reserved`, `test_one_unknown_exit_does_not_stop_the_other_instruments_flatten`, `test_a_raising_admission_behind_a_pause_cannot_wedge_the_kill_queue`, `test_a_kill_exit_queued_behind_a_pause_goes_out_only_at_the_current_price`, `test_a_kill_exit_denied_every_tick_retries_once_per_observation`, `test_kill_to_flat_drains_every_symbol_under_429_pacing_with_a_moving_price`, `test_a_denied_protect_does_not_silence_that_symbols_kill`, `test_a_prepare_error_registers_nothing_and_the_next_tick_flattens_once`, `test_a_native_refusal_on_the_kill_path_spends_no_token_and_waits_for_an_observation`, `test_an_invalid_kill_exit_price_grows_no_bookkeeping_and_never_raises`, `test_a_reopened_writer_reloads_custody_and_never_resells_the_next_writers_exit`; `tests/proofs/test_paper_node.py::test_the_runtime_runs_its_own_hook_before_the_native_command`, `test_an_unexpected_prepare_error_registers_nothing_and_halts`, `test_an_error_once_the_wire_is_counted_is_never_released`, `test_an_admission_error_at_the_drain_halts_and_pops_instead_of_wedging`, `test_a_late_denial_releases_only_this_writers_live_registration`; | Kill-to-flat keeps one CANCEL per house order queued or pending, capped at max_cancel_retries + 1 sends counted in custody, and records a cancel left unanswered through a halt; quantity bounds are refused before the token (#187, #195) | Row 8's bridge `src/sextant/execution/alpaca.py:141-152,305,355-375,408,630-631` (one cancel generation per order; a re-cancel on each read that still shows the order open; `cancel_sends` kept in custody and capped at `max_cancel_retries + 1`, whose registry row cites seathatflowsinourveins/northstar-trading@paper `8256c16c31dcb8e53fc726e0c1c77dc2c8d0d4fa` `src/nautilus_alpaca/order_reducer.py`); nautechsystems/nautilus_trader@`d53849b216e20f2c122ca01b4dd0da377638b186` (2.0.0rc8): `crates/trading/src/strategy/mod.rs:629-631,822-827` (a cancel of an order already pending cancel or closed is logged and dropped), `:891-911` (OrderPendingCancel is stamped with the clock as the cancel leaves), `crates/model/src/orders/mod.rs:257,265,271-272,1020,1380-1386,1402` (a pending modify moves to PENDING_CANCEL; a partial fill, a rejected modify or a voided fill keeps PENDING_CANCEL, and every event moves `ts_last`), `crates/live/src/execution/manager.rs:1935-1939` (after its retries the in-flight check marks a stuck PENDING_CANCEL as CANCELED locally) and `crates/risk/src/engine/mod.rs:1782-1799` (QuantityExceedsMaximum and QuantityBelowMinimum, denied after the token, strict > and <); the #88 gate review of #163 at `bf10c7f` (#187) and the reviews of #190 at `325bcf5` and `66496c4` (#195), and the author review of the #195 fix; ADR-0007, ADR-0009 | During a halt the guard re-emits CANCEL_ENTRY every `cancel_retry_s`, and CANCEL outranks REDUCE in row 8's pacer. For every caller, one CANCEL per house order waits in the queue. `_prepare` refuses, before the token, a CANCEL of an order rc8 would drop because it is already pending cancel or closed; a cancel of an order with a modify in flight still leaves. Because rc8 sends no second cancel, an entry whose cancel nobody answers sends one CANCEL, and the bridge's re-cancel on each read becomes a check each time the CANCEL is asked for again. Once a read taken `cancel_timeout_s` after rc8's latest OrderPendingCancel still shows the order open, the runtime records the unknown outcome, as the bridge does at its cap, and marks the order `cancel_unanswered` in custody. The stamp is that event's, not `ts_last`, which a partial fill moves while the order stays pending. A read showing the order closed refuses the CANCEL first, so nothing is recorded. During a halt the guard asks again every `cancel_retry_s`; outside a halt nothing asks again, so a lost CANCEL waits for the next request or halt (#196). After a restart rc8 keeps no unclaimed house order, so the native layer cannot cancel it (#197). rc8's in-flight check stays off, and a proof pins it, because after its retries it would mark that order CANCELED locally and hide an entry that may still be live. Each CANCEL that leaves is counted in custody (`cancel_sends`), so the cap survives a reopen or a restart; after `max_cancel_retries + 1` the runtime sends no more for that order. Only real sends count, so the cap applies when each cancel is rejected. Deviation from the bridge: at its cap the bridge marks the order UNKNOWN, which freezes its symbol, and `Guard.step` then stops every intent. The runtime instead records the unknown outcome once through its existing `unknown_execution_outcome` halt, keeps an earlier halting cause in `last_error`, and keeps flattening every other position. Custody names each such order durably, by `cancel_sends` above the cap or by `cancel_unanswered`; `last_error` names the first when no earlier cause holds it. The cost is bounded rather than removed. In the row-8 drill with 24 entries and a read every 2 s, unanswered cancels cost a token each: the first kill EXIT goes out at 10 s, the guard's own `cancel_timeout_s` wait, and the record comes at 12 s; with 100 entries both come at 38 s. Rejected cancels spend the cap's four tokens each: the first kill EXIT goes out within 40 s (36 s measured) and the record comes at 24 s. `_prepare` also refuses a quantity outside the instrument's minimum and maximum before the token, matching rc8's strict comparisons. A failed cancel write keeps custody loaded (#198): the `cancel_unanswered` mark is best effort and names its cause in `last_error`, and a CANCEL leaves only with a durable count, so a failed count write halts with its cause named and sends nothing | `tests/proofs/test_paper_row8.py::test_unacknowledged_entry_cancels_cannot_starve_the_kill_exits`, `test_a_read_showing_the_order_closed_never_records_its_cancel`, `test_every_cancel_caller_shares_one_queued_cancel_and_the_durable_cap`; `tests/proofs/test_paper_native.py::test_an_unanswered_cancel_is_recorded_once_a_late_read_still_shows_the_order_open`, `test_a_rejected_then_lost_cancel_ages_from_the_lost_cancels_stamp`, `test_a_cancel_of_a_pending_or_closed_native_order_is_refused_before_the_token`, `test_a_quantity_outside_the_instruments_bounds_is_refused_before_the_token`, `test_risk_and_reconciliation_config_cannot_disable_the_shared_fences`; `tests/proofs/test_paper_node.py::test_only_a_read_cancel_timeout_s_after_the_pending_stamp_records_the_cancel`; `tests/proofs/custody/test_custody.py::test_a_cancel_count_is_durable_and_the_loader_refuses_a_bad_one`, `tests/proofs/test_paper_row8.py::test_a_failed_audit_mark_never_stops_kill_to_flat`, `test_a_failed_cancel_count_sends_nothing_halts_named_and_keeps_flattening` |

## Entry replay

| Practice or pick | Source, pinned | How it is applied | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mover-slot allocation: the recorded 0.4 pool, 2 of 5 and 8 of 20 | seathatflowsinourveins/northstar-trading@8256c16c31dcb8e53fc726e0c1c77dc2c8d0d4fa:src/northstar_trading/strategies/premarket_gap/scanner.py:991-1105; docs/research/2026-11-02-pmg-mover-arming-r2.md, Rows; [ADR-0024](adr/0024-extreme-mover-ranking-and-early-entry.md), Mover arming | `src/sextant/entry/slots.py`: a pure shared-pool selector — explicit `max_armed_symbols`, not candidate-list length, supplies the `ROUND_CEILING` mover reservation (2/5, 3/7, 8/20). Retained membership charges its own pool and survives refreshes; qualified potential fills first, then movers, then unused places pass over with potential first. Qualification preserves the caller's sourced ranking; overlapping names consume one place. Unknown or invalid custody snapshots arm nothing; immutable chosen/mover receipts carry membership forward. No clock, feed, order or sizing; the caller supplies ranked candidates and session receipts after the 20:00 reset | `tests/proofs/test_slots.py` in `just test` and `just prove` |
| Replay entry policy and incumbent risk sizing | seathatflowsinourveins/northstar-lanes@main `00203275944a22655b3e14b221cfde9a265d549a`:`docs/adr/0005-entry.md` N1/N2/N4/N5 and `src/northstar_lanes/contracts.py:Params`; seathatflowsinourveins/northstar-trading@paper `8256c16c31dcb8e53fc726e0c1c77dc2c8d0d4fa`:`src/northstar_trading/strategies/premarket_gap/first_leg.py::trade/_run/breakout`, `entry_sizing.py::size_entry/refresh`, `persist.py::write_json_atomic`, `scanner.py::_expire_first_leg:1769-1783` (the TTL callback calls `_cancel` first; its deadline is min(TTL, window end)), `base.py::_cancel:1728-1754` and `CANCEL_RETRY_NS:101` (the earlier 5 s re-request shape; round 11 replaces it with an immediate retry bounded at four attempts, ADR-0007:27), `first_leg.py:392,399-401` (retained identities), `:506-517` (`_run` starts at the first print), `:550` (refuses `not 0 < bid <= ask`: crossed or one-sided, not locked) and `:552` (the window runs from the flag); nautechsystems/nautilus_trader@v2.0.0rc8 `d53849b216e20f2c122ca01b4dd0da377638b186`:`crates/model/src/data/trade.rs::TradeTick` timestamp fields, `crates/data/src/engine/dispatch.rs::handle_trade/handle_trade_pipeline` sequencing, native Strategy/OrderFactory/BacktestEngine, `crates/trading/src/strategy/mod.rs::cancel_order/mark_order_pending_cancel` and `::expire_gtd_order:2258-2288` and `::cancel_order:600-666` with `mark_order_pending_cancel:818-828` (a pending cancel is never sent twice), `crates/trading/src/python/strategy.rs::dispatch_on_order_event`, `crates/risk/src/sizing.rs`, ATR/RMA/VWAP indicators, `crates/model/src/python/types/price.rs::from_decimal_dp`, checked Bar construction and native order serialization; CPython 3.13.18 [collections](https://docs.python.org/3.13/library/collections.html) deque/namedtuple and [function attributes](https://docs.python.org/3.13/reference/datamodel.html#user-defined-functions), read 2026-11-07; pandas 3.0.6 [`merge_asof`](https://pandas.pydata.org/pandas-docs/version/3.0/reference/api/pandas.merge_asof.html) `allow_exact_matches=False` ("strictly less-than"), read 2026-11-07: a print's ATR uses bars known strictly before its event time, the conservative tie the lead ruled. **Owner exception, verbatim, 2026-11-07: "Grant, refusal only".** It covers only the late-print void and the segment VWAP latch, which refuse entries and never add, resize or reprice an order. Where we looked: rc8's DataEngine sequencing (`crates/data/src/engine/dispatch.rs:117-129` publishes trades with no sequence check; `:471-497` drops late bars under `validate_data_sequence`), pinned Northstar `first_leg.py::trade:367-381` (drops late, duplicate and stale prints; its VWAP is the scanner's, `:618`) and round 4's finding that pure dropping hides a vetoing print; northstar-lanes ADR-0005 N2:74-76 and N5:87-90 at their pins cover only a missing ATR, VWAP or buying-power read, not an incomplete VWAP or an in-span late print. | First leg, held reclaim, risk sizing, durable basis and one native LIMIT episode. A dropped late, stale, clock-skewed, future or out-of-order print, including one behind two bar groups, leaves the segment VWAP untrusted until the next segment (N2 UNKNOWN: no RECLAIM) and restarts an in-span first-leg run: a late print's neighbours are not retained, and one higher than the run turns its successor into N1's lower trade. That is broader than N1 for an equal print, and refusal only; `VOID` journals both, and a floor at each drop's `ts_event` keeps any later print stamped at or before it out of the run. An in-order print one bar group behind takes its exact as-of ATR (`atr_before`) and is admitted. Same-timestamp prints are in order and re-sends within max(`first_leg_window_s`, `first_leg_span_s`) drop, as upstream drops its retained duplicates. No event tape, checkpoints, repair or scoped vetoes. A crossed or one-sided newest quote is UNKNOWN until a valid newer one and never falls back to an older quote; a locked book is admitted, as upstream `first_leg.py:550`. The first-leg window runs from the surge flag (N1:46); arming needs a Decimal or Price quiet price. A first-leg-only mode reads N1's ten rows (Northstar `FirstLegParams.from_row:105-146`), never fires RECLAIM, does not read the absent flow rows, and sizes a measurement-only witness of one `instrument.size_increment` (native `make_qty`, `strategies/ema_cross.py`'s fixed `trade_size`, C1 `witness_entry_measurement_only` #130 6711836609), refused unless MODELED. Fresh in-order latency remains eligible; current/previous ATR scalars exclude exact knowledge-time ties. Only upstream rolling flow queues/counters remain for N5/coverage. Actual callback faults expose the first failure and journal each one; the N4 TTL cancel goes out before its journal row; a failed TTL cancel lookup or send is retried at once, up to four attempts (`CANCEL_ATTEMPTS`, ADR-0007:27's K+1), with a PAGE row each and an owner latch at exhaustion; rc8's pending guard and the local in-flight/same-timestamp guard prevent a second send; every cancellation failure pages, and an expired entry still open at the window end latches; a first-leg deadline at or before now refuses with `first_leg_window_s` before submission. Fresh reset has no historical re-add. Ten first-leg values REVIEWED; 18 UNAVAILABLE. R33 Decimal/native indicator exceptions remain. [ADR-0004](adr/0004-entry.md#replay-evidence) records inspected sources, scope and limits. | `entry/test_void_late.py` run restart and why it is broader than N1, the skewed-veto drop floor, VWAP latch, same-timestamp sweeps, retained-identity re-sends, clock-skew refusal, old-redelivery latch and fresh-latency positives; `entry/test_residual_gate.py` round 8's restored late-block witness, the no-biased-level property and later-reclaim refusal against a clean control; `entry/test_delta_gate.py` round-4 dropped-veto, coverage, TTL and price witnesses; `entry/test_failure_gate.py` actual exception latch and reset allocation failure; `entry/test_failure_ttl.py` accepted/pending-submit/pending-update retirement under failure or void, CANCEL-row, before-cancel, single and one-to-five cache-lookup/cancel faults within the four-attempt bound, four faults latching (pages, window-end latch), pending-cancel and same-timestamp dedupe, the exact window-end refusal, nested-dispatch faults and the first-leg window deadline; `entry/test_first_leg_only.py` ten-row mode, no RECLAIM and the MODELED-only witness; `entry/test_asof_atr.py` episode-shift and straddling-touch refusals and the bar-crossing first leg; `entry/test_residual_gate.py` no RECLAIM reprice after the latch; `entry/test_quote_clock.py` invalid-newest-book UNKNOWN and no fallback reprice; `entry/test_signals.py` flag-anchored window and quiet-price types; `entry/test_atr_ties.py` dispatch/1 ns ties and deliberate out-of-order refusal; `entry/test_asof_atr.py` bar-straddle refusal; `entry/test_late_controls.py` native span/consumed-event controls; `entry/test_segment_state.py` fresh boundary and rolling-flow controls; existing signal, native replay, partial-fill funding, sizing journal, segment, quote-clock and R33 paired VWAP proofs |

One script, `scripts/compact_context.py`, serves both events and `just session-start`'s check; hook stdout stays under the documented 10,000-character cap and every hook command tolerates failure, so a compaction is never blocked. `.claude/settings.json` sets `idleCompaction: true` explicitly, upstream's default, so the backstop is stated; the 200,000-token idle threshold stays upstream's default and is UNCALIBRATED in Orca because the env override is out of scope. `just session-start` asserts the screened keys. ## Native lane runtimes

Issue #62; [adoption evidence, host receipts and limits](research/2026-11-06-native-lane-runtimes.md), [native recipe](../deploy/wsl/lane-runtimes/README.md), [real-call receipts, including unsuccessful attempts](../deploy/wsl/lane-runtimes/smoke-receipts.json). Runtime admission/role authority is seathatflowsinourveins/northstar@`b384d298d217d3f8d9aa83356994629536a34795`: ADR-0006's successor ADR-0070, ADR-0033, ADR-0056, `2026-10-24-runtime-roster-roles.md`, `2026-10-30-runtime-adr-finalization.md`. Runtime pins are release-cooled; the owner explicitly selects current OmniRoute. Codex is not a lane pin: `sextant-codex` reuses the sandbox's Codex 0.167.0 from the Runtimes row above (tag `rust-v0.167.0`, cut 2026-11-05), so it is not release-cooled here. Antigravity is the one retained native runtime here; the dated six-runtime routing smoke remains in the receipts as history. Outgoing model/effort evidence is not a task-quality or private-provider-compute claim.

Before writing lane runtime directories, both shell entry points run `parity.py --check-roots`; managed roots (including skills, configs, profiles, plugin caches and installer directories) must be unlinked and resolve inside their lane. Antigravity also guards its native `~/.gemini` config, plugins, plugin-data trees and MCP config before staging or native calls, including existing plugin/state descendants; the host systemd unit is outside this runtime preflight. Each Python writer repeats its own guard before mkdir, chmod or native calls. ACP/headless use the same recorded-link, installed-target and enabled-bundle check as dsh-tui. A clean shelf cache fetches a new locked SHA and checks it out detached; tracked, untracked and ignored changes are refused before fetching ([#281](https://github.com/seathatflowsinourveins/sextant-archive/issues/281)). A shipped profile that never ran (acp, headless) is initialized by that same `dsh plugin add`; a refusal, a filesystem error, or a failed or stalled call is reported and the next step still runs. Every Sextant dsh launch calls `sextant-dsh`, the keyed launcher's own name, so it needs no PATH order (#255's pattern). The launcher is also linked as `dsh` for AO, and that bare name needs `~/.local/bin` ahead of sextant-agents' own unkeyed `dsh`: `update-orca.sh` and setup's first `orca-ide serve` put it first, as `sextant.user-data`'s login PATH does. dsh-tui runs dsh's JS entry, never a shell launcher, so agents key it with `with-keys.sh` (`docs/start.md`). Playwright and CodeGraph are opt-in jobs everywhere. qmd stays its lexical CLI (M:L56: its MCP `query` reranks with a model). An edited lane file or skill is kept and reported. **Row history, 2026-11-08:** [ADR-0030:36](adr/0030-latest-sota-models.md) retired Hermes, Pi, OMP and OpenCode v2 from routing. This PR deleted their former rows — "Pi, native bulk/RPC lane", "OMP, native specialist lane", "OpenCode v2, native independent harness" and "Hermes, native trusted research lane" — and the install/launch surface they shared: the Pi/OpenCode v2 npm lock, OMP's release binary, Hermes's installer and home, and the `api-launch.sh` switch with its `pi`, `omp`, `opencode`, `opencode2`, `hermes` and `openhands-sdk` names. Their pins remain only in `pins.json`'s `retired_runtimes` and Git. The OpenHands SDK's row above is a REJECT, not a retirement: the blind re-evaluation found no maintained worker surface. No retired runtime is installed or called, and the dated six-runtime smoke receipts remain as history.

**Row history, 2026-11-06:** the owner said, verbatim, **"drop gemini, it was not working"**, relayed by the lead on #62. Removed the former **"Gemini CLI, native Google lane"** row and its active install/launch integration; its retired source was google-gemini/gemini-cli@v0.65.0 `6006c14a4decfe4b891c6263ad6ad5992e404b5b`. No Gemini call is claimed or required. The Google family remains native authenticated Antigravity and OmniRoute's agy/antigravity connections, with ADR-0030's Argon High interim while Carbon is absent from the account catalog. The retired pin is retained only in `pins.json` history and Git.

The bound is the receipt's `timeoutMs` (60 s in Orca 1.4.251); Orca has no global default, so none is invented. ## Public gate

The 2026-11-09 Phase 1 bootstrap targets `sextant-next` and makes `gate` the temporary default only after its old workflows are replaced. These rows describe the gate configuration; hosted proof-run receipts determine whether the rollout is operational. New picks use the 2026-10-25 cutoff. The lead's pin-reuse ruling is recorded below.

| Practice or pick | Source, pinned | How it is applied | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| Default-branch workflow_run gate and unprivileged hosted CI | [GitHub workflow_run](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/events-that-trigger-workflows#workflow_run), [secure use](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/secure-use), read 2026-11-09; actions/github-script@`3a2844b7e9c422d3c10d287c895573f7108da1b3`:`README.md` separate-script and Octokit REST examples; openai/codex@`086ccc833bc9cf01024ad2dd6af7dbd421b6bf9a`:`codex-rs/prompts/templates/review/rubric.md:60-86` | `.github/workflows/ci.yml` fans `lint`, `tests`, `budgets` and `secrets` out as parallel jobs and only reports `ci` when an aggregator job sees all four succeed, on pull_request without repository secrets; the shared `.github/actions/ci-tools/install.sh` script reuses main `.devcontainer/Dockerfile:124-138` versions/checksums via [gh release download](https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_release_download), read 2026-11-09, with only the automatic read-only GitHub job token; `gate.yml` runs only after completed ci, reads the default branch's own files and binds statuses to the triggering head. `.github/gate/gate.cjs` is the REST/event adapter, not a review engine: a quality review passes only on a parseable rubric verdict, and an `overall_correctness: patch is incorrect` verdict with no findings (a review that did not run) fails closed, as do missing and unreadable outputs, with a named reason and no review | `test_public_gate.py::test_no_workflow_requests_a_local_runner_or_privileged_pr_trigger`, `test_gate_runs_only_after_ci_from_the_default_branch`, `test_ci_runs_the_complete_check_without_repository_secrets`, `test_quality_verdict_runs_even_when_the_review_action_fails`, `test_key_policy_and_failure_paths_execute_against_the_actual_adapter`, `test_foundation.py::test_ci_fans_out_into_parallel_jobs_and_the_fan_in_fails_closed` |
| Parallel CI jobs fanned into one required check | [GitHub workflow syntax: `jobs` and `jobs.<job_id>.needs`](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax#jobsjob_idneeds), read 2026-11-09; [zizmor `self-repository` audit](https://docs.zizmor.sh/audits/#self-repository) at zizmor 1.32.0, read 2026-11-09 | `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `lint`, `tests`, `budgets` and `secrets` as independent jobs, then a job named `ci` with `needs: [lint, tests, budgets, secrets]` and `if: always()` fails unless every `toJSON(needs)` result is `success`, keeping the required context name. Its pinned tools come from one shell script, `.github/actions/ci-tools/install.sh`, which `just lint` runs through ShellCheck, so no in-repo action reference exists: the latest actionlint, 1.7.12 of 2026-03-30, rejects GitHub's July-2026 `$/` self-repository form that zizmor 1.32.0 asks for, and a plain script needs neither form | `test_foundation.py::test_ci_fans_out_into_parallel_jobs_and_the_fan_in_fails_closed` |

Distinct reviewed heads the author changed, including the head under review, determine the third-head P0-only safeguard; a merge of the base tip at merge time into the previous head is an update, not an author head, and that tip is matched as the current base tip or one of its ancestors. A clean base-branch merge, whose tree equals the clean merge of its parents, is not an author head and cannot clear the last author head's completed blocking review verdict: the gate carries a `review-blocking-<family>` status forward until an author head is reviewed, and never carries a stale, cancelled, incomplete or infrastructure failure. A merge that also carries author changes has a different tree, is an author head, and its own fresh review decides. `.github/gate/gate.cjs` uses the github-script REST pattern to route upstream outputs and update that issue, without reimplementing dsh-github's verdict pipeline | `test_public_gate.py::test_landing_standard_and_fail_on_error_are_wired_into_the_verdict`, `test_key_policy_and_failure_paths_execute_against_the_actual_adapter` |

## Identity cutover

Phase 2's configs and tests run against `sextant-next`; the old name returns only after P3.7.

| Practice or pick | Source, pinned | How it is applied | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|

## Cutover

The 2026-11-09 cutover keeps every historical receipt reachable: the old repository is archived, and the new repository is the active one.

| Practice or pick | Source, pinned | How it is applied | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| Historical receipts retarget to the archive | [Renaming a repository](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/creating-and-managing-repositories/renaming-a-repository) and [archiving a repository](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/archiving-a-github-repository/archiving-repositories), read 2026-11-09 | Every historical issue, pull request, release, Actions and rules-API locator names `seathatflowsinourveins/sextant-archive`; the four blob links to commits that exist in the new history keep `sextant`; the `candidate_history` release constant and its receipt locator move with the archive tag | `tests/proofs/test_research_claims.py`, `tests/proofs/test_candidate_history.py`, `tests/proofs/test_ranking.py`, `tests/proofs/test_research_sources.py`; `just prove` |
| Workflow branch references derive from the default branch, so the gate-to-main rename needs no workflow edit | [GitHub contexts](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/contexts#github-context): `github.ref` is the fully-formed ref that received the `workflow_dispatch`, and `github.event` carries `repository.default_branch`; [Renaming a branch](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/configuring-branches-and-merges-in-your-repository/managing-branches-in-your-repository/renaming-a-branch): an open pull request's base branch and branch protection follow the rename, while "GitHub Actions workflows do not follow renames"; both read 2026-11-09; the in-repo pattern `.github/workflows/upkeep.yml:35,300`; the lead's 2026-11-09 cutover ruling A1 | `.github/workflows/dsh-worker.yml:26` guards the job on `github.ref == format('refs/heads/{0}', github.event.repository.default_branch)` and `:188` opens the pull request with `base: ${{ github.event.repository.default_branch }}`, the pattern `upkeep.yml` already uses; the file names no branch, so a dispatched run on any other branch is skipped and the rename needs no edit | `test_dsh_worker.py::test_one_hosted_job_per_owner_dispatch_on_the_default_branch`, `test_dsh_worker.py::test_cpr_uses_the_app_token` |

## Watching, not installed
Each has a named trigger. Source for ECC and the two plugins: the three-family reconciliation cited above, and the DeepSeek leg at the same commit (`docs/research/2026-11-04-cc-native-practice-legs/cc-native-practice/leg-deepseek.json`).
- **affaan-m/ECC v2.2.3:** the DeepSeek leg measured 68 agents, 293 skills and 94 commands, which conflicts with "never bloat". Trigger: the owner asks for it, or a named practice in it has no other source.
- **mksglu/context-mode:** trigger: a measured need in this repo.
- **ARIS, Hindsight, OpenHands:** triggers in [the foundations convergence](research/2026-11-05-foundations-convergence.md).
- **eve1329/dsh-shared-handoff** (MIT): `.agents/state` shared with Codex and Claude, handoff and per-turn snapshots. Trigger: a measured cross-family handoff that lost state; then a leg, a row and its proof. Screen: [handbook/plugins.md](handbook/plugins.md).
- **kenz1117/dsh-engram** (MIT): SQLite FTS5 plus vector retrieval, a provenance chain, SM-2 and decay. Trigger: OpenViking is retired or a measured retrieval need remains; then a leg, a row and its proof. Never two memories. Screen: [handbook/plugins.md](handbook/plugins.md).

## Third-party plugins

A plugin's code runs with the session's permissions, so a catalog listing is not a review ([handbook/plugins.md](handbook/plugins.md)). A row here records all five: its pin, its capability and red-line disclosure at that pin, its one owner, its proof and its removal. No row, no install; an unscanned plugin is unavailable, never clean. `dangerfile.js` refuses a named row in this section that omits its disclosure.

| Practice or pick | Source, pinned | How it is applied | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| dsh-test-autofix: the run_tests repair loop, interactive dsh-tui only | **Pin:** [1250589385/dsh-test-autofix](https://github.com/1250589385/dsh-test-autofix)@`2246371b2869db52c0f9a77098777d66dbdbad32` (MIT, package 0.1.3, committed 2026-10-23, past the 14-day cooldown): `README.md` install (`dsh plugin --profile <profile> add github:1250589385/dsh-test-autofix`), `index.js:888-915` (injects `tools`, registers the one `run_tests` tool). **Disclosure:** [liuwenji007/dsh-trust-check](https://github.com/liuwenji007/dsh-trust-check)@`787912e37367e3b0e5cbe5a259253a88f3afd502` on the pinned tree, 2026-11-10T21:33Z: `dsh-test-autofix 0.1.3 · yellow 58/100 · shell, fs-read, env` ("review suggested"); capabilities shell, fs-read and env; red lines none; no network destination, secret touch, file write, or install, build or prepare script. outside RTH it reports each held symbol `protected=0` and `trigger=none` unless the last successful poll is inside the registry `feed_silence_s` and that symbol has a quote; a standing quote stays armed through a quiet market and the line reports both `poll_age` and `quote_age`. A release is priced under the same predicate, so a standing quote, or one stamped after the breach print, prices the LIMIT; a stopped poll or a held symbol with no quote pages `outside_rth_feed_silent` once per episode. Observations stamped before the session opened are rejected, the breach run restarts at each session/feed boundary, and the native trail's stop is adopted before the first quote of a new session. At process start only, in any session, before the stream's loop exists and on its thread, the protector reads each held position's tape once: the adoption instant is B's first house trailing stop (never a released LIMIT) submitted at or after the fill time of the latest quantity-increasing owner fill, and a buy that is not fully filled has no fill time (alpaca-py 0.44.0 `alpaca/trading/models.py:178,217`: `filled_at: Optional[datetime]`, "Timestamp when the order was filled"; [Alpaca's order reference](https://docs.alpaca.markets/us/reference/getallorders-1), read by this change's writer 2026-11-10, gives `filled_at` no description and no partial-fill meaning), so one last updated after the latest full fill makes the entry unknown (a restart is not an adoption, an owner add is; with no such trail B adopts now, reads nothing and claims no tape status); only the pre-market, post-market and overnight spans since then are read, since the resting native trail carries the regular-hours high, each on its own session feed (SIP pre/post, BOATS overnight) as one raw `RESTClient.get` of `/stocks/quotes` per page, newest first (`sort=desc`, alpaca-py `alpaca/data/requests.py:40`), following `next_page_token` with `page_token` as the SDK's own page loop does (`alpaca/common/rest.py:381,388,398`), each page one request inside the same deadline and budget (alpaca-py `alpaca/common/rest.py:212`, `alpaca/data/historical/stock.py:98-118`), so one account-pacer admission is one HTTP request with no SDK page loop or retry sleep (a refused read, positions and orders included, is withdrawn and never sent later); one hard elapsed `tape_deadline_s` and a `tape_max_requests` budget bound the whole read, trading calls included (a request starts only while one `tape_read_timeout_s` fits before the deadline, and a main-thread `signal.setitimer(ITIMER_REAL)` whose handler raises ends the read at it, per the [signal module's example](https://docs.python.org/3/library/signal.html) read 2026-11-10 and PEP 475, always disarmed with the previous handler and timer restored; off the main thread nothing is read and one page is sent; every position not completed is partial with its page; the first reconcile so begins within `tape_deadline_s`), each request is bounded by `tape_read_timeout_s` (requests' transport timeout on a mounted `HTTPAdapter`, on a start-only trading client too) and a symbol stops at its first failure; completeness is judged on the raw JSON (a window is complete at its page without a token; a page over `tape_max_quotes`, a null or malformed row or a malformed token is partial; a one-sided row is skipped) and the quotes are replayed through the same rc8 BID_ASK calculator; the rebuilt level applies at the first outside-hours adoption as the maximum of every valid source, never below any: the resting native stop, the level the trail's own `hwm` and `trail_percent` imply (alpaca-py `alpaca/trading/models.py:206,208,242,244`; a missing stop still pages) and the read's level, whole when `tape=ok` and from a `tape=partial` read the lower bound its rows since adoption imply (the pages read before the deadline or budget ended), only while its quantity equals the held quantity, and a quantity change drops the read in every session; `tape=ok` needs every window read to its last page with well-formed responses for that symbol, any failure, malformed shape or bound is `tape=partial` with one `outside_rth_tape_unreadable` page, and no fill time for the latest owner buy is `tape=entry_unknown` with its own `outside_rth_entry_unknown` page; a position adopted while B runs needs no read, and raising the trigger keeps the breach run. Outside RTH `on_quote` ratchets the emulated BID_ASK trigger through the rc8 calculator and `on_trade` counts the registry `breach_persist_count`/`breach_persist_span_s`; a persistent run cancels the house trail, and only that trail's terminal cancellation starts the handover: it re-reads positions and reservations, re-prices from the freshest quote, and releases one marketable `LimitOrderRequest` with `extended_hours=True` and GTC at the registry `chase_limit` price collared to the sub-penny ladder, its price crossing as `str(Decimal)`; a failed submit re-arms the emulation; the released LIMIT is left alone while it matches the whole shares; the BID_ASK trigger uses the sub-dollar increment below $1.00 so a sub-penny stop is not rounded up onto the penny grid; every exit decision (an adoption, a release's resolution and any other cancel of a house exit) also appends one JSON line to the run's `state/b-decisions.jsonl` under the config directory, off the hot path on one writer thread and fsynced per line, naming the decision time, the symbol, the trigger level and which source raised it (native stop, broker hwm, tape level with its window/feed/pages/status, or the live quote with its bid/ask/time), the high-water mark with its quote, the completing observations with their policy noise, the order and the position/quantity bookkeeping, all decimal money as strings; a write failure or a full queue pages `decision_record_write_failed` from a pager thread started before protection begins (Python logging cookbook read 2026-11-10: `QueueHandler`/`QueueListener`, the non-blocking handler shape), its backlog bounded at 64 pages with overflow counted, and the protective thread never starts or waits on a thread, so a stalled or unstartable sink cannot hold or abort the exit and no decision changes; ## B paper entry facade and ledger

| Practice or pick | Source, pinned | How it is applied | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| B pending entry reserve follows its client order ID | alpacahq/alpaca-py@v0.44.0 `cc4cb3b7ba50ae250e621983c2779047fb16bb28`:`alpaca/trading/client.py:157-174` (lookup by retained identity), `alpaca/trading/enums.py:148-169` (order states); northstar-trading@`8256c16c31dcb8e53fc726e0c1c77dc2c8d0d4fa`:`src/northstar_trading/strategies/premarket_gap/first_leg.py:392,399-401` (retained identities); ADR-0007 C4 | The account pacer retains one charge per admitted ID before the POST, including lost acknowledgements; pending charges join held positions in the full protective reserve. A same-ID retry never creates or clears a charge. Missing, empty or blank IDs refuse before admission; malformed optional controls leave entries unavailable while protection starts. Only that ID's terminal receipt or explicit 429 rejection settles it, with a tombstone preventing resurrection; 504 remains uncertain. An exceptional drain withdraws an unsent entry without charging it. Snapshot/lookup and stream receipts settle idempotently, without extra reads or owner-order charges. Per-position accounting stays: a full rearm of n positions takes 2+4n wires, so burst 18 admits held counts 0-3 and refuses 4+. | `test_b_entry_charge_ledger.py::test_charge_identity_invariant_over_seeded_event_sequences`, `::test_same_id_retry_and_lost_ack_preserve_the_original_charge`, `::test_raised_protective_drain_withdraws_unsent_entry_without_a_phantom_charge`, `::test_terminal_receipts_only_settle_their_admitted_identity_and_never_resurrect_it`, `::test_one_reconcile_has_one_pair_of_account_reads`, `::test_full_rearm_counts_each_terminal_reconcile`, `::test_burst_18_admission_table_keeps_the_per_position_read_charge`; `test_b_entry_reserve_review.py`, `test_b_entry_facade_sdk.py` |

## B entry own partial-fill protection reserve

| Practice or pick | Source, pinned | How it is applied | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| B entry partial/full protection and definitive rejection settlement | northstar-trading@`8256c16c31dcb8e53fc726e0c1c77dc2c8d0d4fa`:`src/northstar_trading/execution/pacer.py`; alpaca-py@v0.44.0 `cc4cb3b7ba50ae250e621983c2779047fb16bb28`:`alpaca/trading/enums.py:148-169`, `alpaca/common/rest.py:195-207`; ADR-0007 C4 and `lanes/custody-parameters.json` reviewed `reserve_per_open_position`/`reserve_per_exiting_position` (2/1, UNCALIBRATED) | Measure the entry's partial fill, full fill and terminal cancel: three snapshot pairs, two trail submissions and a cancel, nine wires at the existing rows. Add two snapshot pairs plus the exiting write floor for each new/pending entry to the earlier reserve; a pending-ID count is supplied separately from the held count. At burst 18 a fresh entry with 0/1/2 held requires 9/13/17 remaining tokens, and 3 held or a second unsettled entry refuses. No new tunable or registry value. Definitive HTTP 403/422/429 settles only its charge; timeout, 5xx or missing response retains exposure. F3/F4 stay with issue #351. | `test_b_entry_partial_reserve.py` ports the Opus 18c6226 probes with asserted coverage, funded-boundary wire measurements, two pending entries, rejection identity isolation and ambiguous dispatches; `test_b_entry_charge_ledger.py` retains all 6144 operations and assertions with its four-ID stress bucket derived from five measured paths, not B deployment authority |
| Nautilus ALPACA market data for the sandbox Strategy | [NautilusTrader Python adapter guide](https://github.com/nautechsystems/nautilus_trader/blob/d53849b216e20f2c122ca01b4dd0da377638b186/docs/developer_guide/python_adapters.md), v2.0.0rc8 `d53849b216e20f2c122ca01b4dd0da377638b186`: `examples/live/_template/{data,factories}.py`, `python/nautilus_trader/live/{clients.py,__init__.pyi}`, [Architect AX row parsing](https://github.com/nautechsystems/nautilus_trader/blob/d53849b216e20f2c122ca01b4dd0da377638b186/crates/adapters/architect_ax/src/data.rs#L1438-L1473); alpaca-py v0.44.0 `cc4cb3b7ba50ae250e621983c2779047fb16bb28`: `alpaca/data/{live/stock.py,live/websocket.py,historical/stock.py,requests.py}`; [Alpaca real-time stock data](https://docs.alpaca.markets/us/docs/real-time-stock-pricing-data), [historical bars](https://docs.alpaca.markets/us/reference/stockbars), [Python 3.13 call_soon_threadsafe](https://docs.python.org/3.13/library/asyncio-eventloop.html#asyncio.loop.call_soon_threadsafe), read 2026-11-10; [ADR-0013](adr/0013-us-equity-data-stack.md) | `src/sextant/alpaca/data.py` publishes typed quote, trade and minute-bar data from one IEX or SIP SDK stream. Handler changes run on the hosted stream loop and serialize with the SDK's initial and reconnect subscription sends. Typed publication returns to the node loop. The client drops and counts invalid spreads and unconvertible streamed quotes, trades and bars, logging each conversion exception on the node loop. It caps exchange-qualified trade IDs at rc8’s 36-character limit. Synchronous SDK history runs off-loop and returns correlated quote, trade and minute/day bar responses. The E2 factory supplies the provider and names-only configuration. | `tests/proofs/test_alpaca_data.py`, `just prove` |
